### Page 2

足元の経済・金融状況と注目点
1
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
2026. 11.17
米国では物価高で実質賃金が伸び悩む一方、
カード延滞率に改善の兆し。NY連銀によれば、
延滞率の高止まりはコロナ期の与信拡大が影響
しており、今後も緩やかな改善が続くとの見立て
米家計のクレジットカード延滞率に改善の兆し
米国
10月の消費者物価は市場予想を上回る結果。生産
者物価も高止まり。市場は12月利上げを織り込み。
一方で、金利上昇を背景に中古住宅販売は大幅減。
根強いインフレ圧力と住宅市場の弱さが鮮明に
欧州
ユーロ圏の7～9月期GDPは前期から減速。ドイツ
は小幅プラスも、フランスはマイナス、スペインは堅
調と主要国でばらつき。ユーロ圏全体では雇用の伸
びも横ばいとなっており、景気回復力の鈍さを確認
中国
10月の輸出は高い伸びを維持。半導体や自動車な
どがけん引。一方で輸入も大幅増。生産は輸出堅調
で底堅く推移も、投資は下落幅が拡大し、消費も力
強さ欠き低迷。消費者物価も前月から伸びが鈍化

日本
9月の実質消費支出は10カ月連続のマイナス。景気
ウォッチャー調査も6カ月ぶりに悪化し、家計部門の
弱さを示唆する内容。国内企業物価は原油高などを
受けて再び加速。今後消費者物価へ波及する公算
注目点①
日本の消費は所得の伸びを下回る緩慢な回復。
一方、大型M&Aなどから企業の資金需要は旺盛
で、銀行貸出は高い伸びを維持。預金獲得競争
が激化するなか、安定的な預金調達が重要に
日本の家計は消費に慎重、企業は借入に積極的
注目点②

米国：物価高で実質賃金は伸び悩むも、クレジットカード延滞率に改善の兆し
 2026年10月のコアCPI（食品・エネルギーを除く）は前月比＋0.3％と市場予想（同＋0.2％）を上回る結果
―3カ月移動平均では2025年1月以来の高水準。財・サービス価格ともに上昇が続きインフレ圧力の根強さを確認
― 名目賃金の増加が物価上昇に相殺され、実質平均時給は前月比横ばい。家計の購買力は伸び悩み
 一方、クレジットカードの90日以上延滞への新規移行率は高水準ながらも小幅に低下
―7～9月期は6.87％と前年同期の7.05％を下回り、家計の信用状況には改善の兆し
3
（出所）米国労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：カード延滞率の高さは足元の所得環境だけではなく、過去の与信拡大の影響を反映
 NY連銀によれば、コロナ禍には信用力の低い借り手へのカード発行が拡大
― 全体の信用スコアが改善する中、新規発行時の信用スコア中央値は低下。リスクの高い層への与信を増やした格好
 当時発行されたカードがその後の延滞率上昇に大きく寄与
― ただし、コロナ期の与信拡大による影響は徐々にはく落。加えて、2023年以降は新規発行時の信用力が改善しており、
雇用・所得環境が大きく悪化しなければ、今後も延滞率の緩やかな改善が続く公算
4

日本：個人消費は所得の伸びを下回る緩慢な拡大。先行き不安が回復の重石に
5
 個人消費は拡大基調を維持も、所得の伸びを下回る緩慢な拡大
―9月の消費活動指数は前年比＋0.8％と拡大基調を維持したものの、耐久財特需のはく落などから増勢は鈍化
― 実質雇用者報酬が着実に増加する一方、消費の伸びは所得を下回る状況が継続
 先行き不安も家計の慎重な支出姿勢に影響
― 消費者マインドは食品やエネルギーなど身近な消費財の価格上昇に対する懸念から低調に推移
― 中東情勢を巡る不透明感もあり、当面の消費は緩慢な拡大にとどまる見通し

日本：大型M&Aなどもあり銀行貸出は高い伸び。金利上昇下でも企業の資金需要は底堅く推移
6
 企業の資金需要は旺盛。銀行貸出は高い伸びを維持
―10月の貸出残高（銀行・信用金庫計）は前年比＋5.2％と都市銀行を中心に高い伸び。とりわけ、大企業の資金需要が
旺盛。中堅・中小企業の資金需要にも底堅さ。活発な企業買収も資金需要の追い風に
 金利上昇下でも当面の資金需要は底堅い見通し
― 日銀の利上げに伴い新規貸出金利は上昇も、投資需要などを背景に企業の借入需要は底堅く推移する見込み
― なお、地域銀行を中心に預貸率が上昇。メガバンクを含め預金獲得競争が激化するなか、安定的な預金調達が重要に

（電力価格、2026年1～8月累積、前年同期比、％）
米国：民主党勝利によるデータセンター規制の強化は電力価格の上昇を抑制する可能性
 米国では家庭向け電力価格が上昇。背景に送配電設備の更新・増強や異常気象
― 足元では、データセンター（DC）稼働数と電力価格の上昇率に明確な関係はみられず
 一方、DC増設に伴う将来の電気料金負担増や水資源への影響を懸念し、規制強化を求める動きが拡大
― 中間選挙では、DC規制の強化を掲げる民主党知事候補が各州で勝利。とりわけ、DCの集積が進むテキサス・オハイ
オ・ジョージアでは、長年共和党が知事職を握ってきたものの、民主党候補が勝利する結果。今後、DC開発を巡る規制
が強化され、家計への費用転嫁が抑えられれば、中長期的な電力価格の上昇ペースは緩和される可能性
7
（注） 青色はオハイオ州、テキサス州、ジョージア州

主な方針州・当選者
条件を満たさないDCの建設を停止。 事業者
に電気・ガス・水道の費用を全額負担させ、
労働組合に加入する労働者による建設、情
報公開、環境基準の遵守などを義務付ける
方針
オハイオ
エイミー・ア
クトン氏
新規DCの承認を一時停止。 住民が建設の
是非に関与できる法律が整うまで、新規案件
を認めない。DC向けの税優遇を廃止し、送
電網の増強費用などを一般家庭に転嫁させ
ない方針
テキサス
ジーナ・ヒノ
ホサ氏
州全体で新規DC建設を一時停止。 電気料
金や水資源への影響を調査し、建設ルール
を整備。DC向けの税優遇を廃止し、電力設
備の整備費用を事業者に負担させる方針

欧州：ユーロ圏経済はプラス成長継続。一方、フランスでは不安定な政治情勢が投資の重石に
 ユーロ圏全体の7～9月期GDPは潜在成長率（約＋1.2％）を上回る伸びを維持
― アイルランドを除くと年率＋0.8％成長。スペインは内需主導で高成長維持も、フランスがマイナス転化する等国ごとに濃淡
 フランスの不安定な政治情勢が景気に暗雲。設備投資の下押し要因に
― フランスでは不安定な政治情勢が経済の不確実性を高めるなか、民間設備投資の足踏みが続く
― 同国の10月企業景況感は前月から大幅に悪化し、縮小圏に転落。先行きも、政治・財政の不透明感の長期化や金融環境
の引き締まりを背景に、一部業種（情報通信サービス等）を除き低調に推移する見込み
8

中国：投資は下落幅が拡大し、消費も力強さを欠き低迷。生産は輸出堅調で底堅く推移

― 生産は輸出堅調で底堅く推移（前月：同＋5.5％⇒10月：同＋4.8％）。自動車が同＋0.9％と、内需低迷で伸びず

― 先行きは、信用スプレッド拡大など金融環境のタイト化を通じたAI投資の選別・減速が、アジアの輸出拡大に波及するリ

### Page 14

日本：7～9月期実質GDPは横ばい圏の推移。消費低調の一方、設備投資が堅調に回復
 2026年7～9月期の実質GDP（1次速報値）は前期比▲0.0％（年率▲0.1％）とほぼ横ばい
― 個人消費が2四半期連続でマイナスとなったほか、輸出が一時的要因もあり小幅減となり、成長率を下押し。一方、設備
投資はAI関連需要の堅調さを背景にプラス転換し、成長率を押し上げ
―4～6月期（前期比＋0.4％）から減速し、経済の緩やかな拡大傾向が一服
 今後、2026年度後半の実質GDPは、均してみれば小幅なプラス成長と予想
― 個人消費が精彩を欠く一方で、設備投資の拡大が支えになる構図が継続。2026年度全体では＋1％弱の成長率に

## Document 062: 2026年10月经济数据点评：新兴产业与外需支撑景气，内需修复持续承压

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月经济数据点评：新兴产业与外需支撑景气，内需修复持续承压.pdf
Report source: 西南证券
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分  
1 
[Table_ReportInfo] 2026 年 11 月 16 日      
证 券研究报告•宏观简评报告     
数据点评 
新兴产业与外需支撑景气，内需修复持续承压 
——2026 年 10 月经济数据点评

点评

西 南证券研究院 
[Table_Summary]  摘要：工业生产方面，10 月规模以上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，增速较上月
回落 0.7 个百分点，低于市场预期。高技术制造业和装备制造业增加值分别增
长 17.6%、10.6%，其中高技术制造业创近五年来单月增速新高，AI相关电子
硬件、工业机器人、3D 打印设备等产品产量保持较快增长。同时，10 月规上
工业出口交货值增长 18.6%，外需仍是工业生产的重要支撑。整体工业生产呈
现总量放缓、结构分化的特征，高技术制造和出口链条景气度较高，传统周期
性行业修复相对滞后。固定资产投资方面，1-10 月全国固定资产投资同比下降
8.1%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.9 个百分点，投资需求仍处于深度收缩区间。基建
投资方面，1-10 月广义基建投资下降 5.1%，专项债发行节奏放缓、资金向实
物工作量转化存在时滞，整体托底效应尚未充分显现，但互联网和相关服务业
投资，以及交通运输、电力供应等重点领域投资仍保持正增长。随着专项债剩
余额度加快使用及“两重”项目建设推进，重点基建领域投资有望获得一定支
撑，但扭转整体投资下行趋势仍面临较大难度。房地产投资方面，1-10 月房地
产开发投资同比下降 21.6%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 1.0 个百分点，地产仍是整体
投资的重要拖累。供给端施工、竣工面积单月同比分别下降 32.5%、39.1%，
降幅均有所扩大，新开工降幅有所收窄但仍处负增长区间；需求端商品房销售
面积、销售额分别下降 20.7%、20.5%，降幅小幅收窄，但销售修复尚未有效
传导至房企投资和施工活动。房企资金来源继续收缩，行业投资意愿和项目建
设规模仍然偏弱，预计年内地产投资仍处深度调整阶段，降幅收窄时点或推迟
至 2027 年。制造业投资方面，1-10 月制造业投资同比下降 2.9%，降幅较 1-9
月扩大 0.8 个百分点，企业资本开支意愿仍然偏弱。高技术制造业投资增长
3.0%，电子电路、锂离子电池和集成电路制造业投资增速较快，科技产业投资
仍具韧性。“两新”政策继续支撑设备更新，设备工器具购置投资增长 8.9%，
占全部投资比重提升至 20.3%。但汽车、通用设备等装备制造行业投资明显走
弱，反映生产端韧性尚未转化为新增资本开支。消费方面，10 月社会消费品
零售总额同比增长 0.4%，增速较上月回落 0.7 个百分点，消费需求整体偏弱。
汽车类零售同比下降 21.4%，仍是社零增长的主要拖累；通讯器材、金银珠宝
等品类受前期高基数及金价波动影响，零售增速明显回落，家电、家具消费则
受低基数影响边际改善。服务消费相对更具韧性，1-10 月服务零售额增长
4.9%，高于商品零售额 4.0 个百分点。往后看，汽车及地产相关消费能否改善、
以旧换新政策效应能否延续，仍是影响商品消费修复的重要因素，服务消费则
有望继续成为居民消费的重要支撑。 
 工业生产总量边际降温，科技制造与出口链条维持高景气。2026 年 10 月，全
国规模以上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，增速较上月收窄 0.7 个百分点，低于
wind 一致预期的 5.32%。分行业看，采矿业、制造业、电力、热力、燃气及
水生产和供应业的工业增加值同比较上月均边际走弱，增速分别回落至 0.7%、
5.4%、4.4%。但制造业工业增加值同比相比近十年季节性水平略高，表明生
产端并未出现明显失速。结构上，10 月高技术产业、装备制造业工业增加值
同比分别增长 17.6%、10.6%。高技术产业增速创下近 5 年来单月最高增速，
其中电子工业专用设备制造、计算机外围设备制造、电子器件制造等行业增加
值分别增长 46.8%、44.7%、34.8%，显示电子信息产业链仍处于较高景气区
间。装备制造业 1-10 月累计增加值占全部规模以上工业比重为 37.9%，同比
提高 1.8 个百分点。细分行业中，10 月单月，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制

2
造业、专用设备制造业、石油和天然气开采业、铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他
运输设备制造业增加值同比分别增长 19.4%、11.5%、9.4%、9.2%，在主要
行业中增幅居前。金属制品业（8.6%）、食品制造业（8.6%）、医药制造业（5.9%）
增速均边际回升。非金属矿物制品业（-2.3%）、煤炭开采和洗选业（-4.4%）
同比降幅均小幅收窄。这反映出工业生产的改善并非全面扩散，传统周期性行
业仍受到需求不足、价格偏弱等因素制约，产业间景气分化较为明显。从产品
看，“人工智能+”带动产业链上下游产量较快增长，既体现在集成电路
（+21.9%）、电子元件（+15.2%）等核心硬件持续扩张，也体现在工业机器人
（+26.0%）、 3D 打印设备（+85.9%）等高端制造设备加快放量，以及地面通
信导航定向设备（+61.5%）等智能终端产品快速增长，反映出 AI产业发展正
由底层硬件向智能制造和多元化应用场景延伸。出口亦对工业生产形成正向带
动。10 月规模以上工业企业出口交货值同比增长 18.6%，增速较上月加快 1.6

个百分点。装备制造业出口交货值增长 20.6%，增速较上月加快 3.4 个百分点，
其中，汽车、电气机械、电子行业出口交货值分别增长 52.2%、23.7%、15.3%，
对全部规上工业出口交货值增长的贡献率居各大类行业前三位，合计达
66.7%，说明出口对工业生产的拉动并未明显减弱，且支撑力量主要集中在装
备制造和电子产业链。综合来看，年内工业增加值增速或温和放缓，结构升级
趋势明确，高技术制造业和装备制造业是核心支撑。在强出口和稳投资政策发
力下，单月增速有望小幅回升，但难以扭转全年较前三季度小幅放缓的格局。
 整体投资降幅进一步扩大，重点基建领域维持较高景气。2026 年 1-10 月，全
国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 8.1%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.9 个百分
点。根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月单月投资同比下降 17.84%，降幅较上月扩
大 8.16 个百分点。累计降幅扩大相对温和，但单月降幅明显加深，反映近期
投资下行压力仍在加大。基建投资方面，1-10 月广义基建投资同比下降 5.1%，

降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.9 个百分点；根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月单月广义基建
投资同比下降 17.63%，降幅较上月扩大 7.15 个百分点。资金端来看，10 月
地方政府新增专项债发行 3280 亿元，同比涨幅收窄至 14.13%，发行进度
88.07%，慢于去年同期 2.03 个百分点。专项债发行节奏放缓，叠加前期资金
向项目建设转化存在时滞，基建投资尚未形成回升趋势。尽管广义基建投资整
体负增长，但重点领域投资仍保持较快增长。1-10 月互联网和相关服务业投资
同比增长 48.4%，增速较 1-9 月份加快 1.7 个百分点，反映数字基础设施建设
仍有较强投资需求；航空运输业、水上运输业投资均增长 12.5%，电力供应业
投资增长 9.9%，说明“两重”项目建设及“六张网”相关领域投资仍在推进。
往后看，在四季度财政资金集中拨付、重大项目加速形成实物工作量的支撑下，
11、12 月单月投资增速环比有望小幅改善，带动全年降幅较 1-10 月略有收窄，
但全年转正的概率较低。基建投资中部分结构性高增领域可能受低基数、项目

集中开工影响，持续性需观察。同时，专项债剩余额度在年内可能对基建形成
一定支撑，但不宜高估其扭转深度负增的能力。
 地产供需两端低位运行，投资意愿与在建规模仍偏弱。2026 年 1-10 月，全国
房地产开发投资同比下降 21.6%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 1.0 个百分点；据历史数
据倒推测算，10月单月房地产开发投资同比下降 34.24%，降幅较上月扩大 7.04
个百分点，地产对整体投资的拖累有所加深，是投资端最大拖累项之一。供给
端，根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月房屋施工面积同比下降 32.5%，降幅较上
月扩大 13.9 个百分点，反映在建规模明显收缩；新开工面积同比下降 18.0%，
降幅收窄 8.1 个百分点，是供给端唯一边际改善项。新开工绝对增速仍深度负
增，且可能受低基数、项目集中开工影响，房企投资意愿仍未实质性修复；竣
工面积同比下降 39.1%，降幅扩大 2.4 个百分点，反映项目交付端压力或边际
上升。需求端，根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月商品房销售面积同比下降 20.7%，

降幅较上月收窄 1.9 个百分点；销售额同比下降 20.5%，降幅收窄 0.6 个百分
点。销售额降幅收窄幅度小于销售面积，意味着销售均价同比表现相对偏弱，
销售端的量价修复仍不充分。同时，销售端边际改善与投资、施工、竣工端恶
化形成背离。说明销售回款尚未有效转化为房企拿地、开工和施工强度，房企
流动性和投资意愿仍是核心约束。资金端，据历史数据倒推测算，10 月单月
房地产开发资金来源同比下降 22.0%，降幅较上月进一步扩大。其中国内贷款、
自筹资金、定金及预收款分别下降 33.0%、22.0%、15.6%，资金链仍在全面

3
收缩。往后看，年内稳地产政策以公积金改革、核心城市限购优化、购房贴息
等需求端工具为主，供给端主要依赖收储去库存，年底地产投资降幅仍在寻顶，
降幅收窄时点或推迟到 2027 年。
 整体企业资本开支意愿偏弱，高技术制造投资稳健增长。2026 年 1-10 月，制
造业投资同比下降 2.9%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.8 个百分点；据历史数据倒推
测算，10 月单月制造业投资同比下降 9.32%，降幅较上月扩大 8.38 个百分点。
结合本月工业生产数据看，当前生产端的增长尚未充分转化为企业新增资本开
支，制造业投资意愿相对谨慎。分行业看，1-10 月高技术制造业投资同比增长
3.0%，其中电子电路制造业投资增长 59.6%，锂离子电池、集成电路制造业
投资分别增长 18.0%、13.8%；高技术服务业投资增长 5.9%，其中与人工智
能等新兴产业密切相关的信息服务业投资增长 20.5%，显示科技产业投资持续
向电子信息制造和人工智能相关服务领域集中。“两新”政策持续落地显效，

设备购置投资规模不断扩大。1-10 月，设备工器具购置投资同比增长 8.9%；
占全部投资的比重为 20.3%，比上年同期提高 3.2 个百分点。10 月单月，金属
制品业、有色金属冶炼及压延加工业投资增速同比分别下降 8.5%和 2.5%，降
幅较上月分别收窄 8.2 和 6.8 个百分点。铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设
备制造业、汽车制造业、通用设备制造业当月投资同比分别下降 0.2%、21.8%、
21.2%，降幅分别扩大 12.7、13.6、23.5 个百分点。装备制造业内部投资分化
加剧，终端需求偏弱、价格竞争加剧、产能利用率不足，以及前期抢出口和“以
旧换新”带来的需求前置，可能共同影响企业扩产意愿，促使企业资本开支趋
于谨慎。对比来看，10 月装备制造业工业增加值仍增长 10.6%，反映出当前
装备制造业呈现生产保持较强韧性、投资扩张意愿不足的特征，生产端与投资
端的背离进一步加深。往后看，高技术制造业投资动能有望延续，四季度制造
业投资单月同比有望转正，但全年增速明显转正难度较大；设备购置、电子电

路、锂电、集成电路和 AI 相关信息服务是主要支撑，汽车、通用设备和传统
制造或成为主要拖累。
 消费总量增长继续放缓，部分品类受到价格和低基数扰动。10 月单月，社会
消费品零售总额同比增长 0.4%，增速较上月收窄 0.7 个百分点；扣除汽车后
的消费品零售额同比增长 2.5%，增速较上月收窄 1.4 个百分点。分项看，商
品零售同比增长 0.3%，增速较上月收窄 0.7 个百分点；餐饮收入同比增长
0.7%，增速收窄 1.4 个百分点，整体消费需求仍偏弱。分品类看，汽车类零售
额同比下降 21.4%，降幅较上月扩大 0.2 个百分点，成为社零增长的主要拖累，
反映居民大额耐用品消费意愿不足。通讯器材类零售同比增长 6.5%，增速较
上月大幅回落 24.6 个百分点，说明前期高增长后基数效应明显，补贴效应和
换机需求均有所减弱。金银珠宝类零售同比下降 28.7%，降幅较上月扩大 19.1
个百分点，主要因近期金价回调压低零售额读数，价格因素大于需求因素，也
反映出金银珠宝消费对金价波动高度敏感。石油及制品类零售同比由下降转为

上升 4.4%，主要受近期油价上涨叠加基数因素影响。家用电器和音像器材类
零售同比上升 2.5%，家具类零售同比下降 2.6%，增速均边际回升，或受去年
“以旧换新”退潮带来的低基数影响。尽管消费总量增速有所回落，消费结构
升级和服务消费韧性仍在延续。1-10 月，社会消费品和服务零售总额同比增长
2.5%，其中服务零售额增长 4.9%，高于商品零售额 4.0 个百分点。往后看，
以旧换新政策边际效应减弱、居民消费意愿回升偏慢等因素或持续拖累社零整
体增速。年内消费韧性更多取决于服务消费能否延续较快增速，以及即时零售、
银发经济等新增长点能否对冲传统商品消费偏弱的拖累，托举社零中枢维持在
温和区间。
 风险提示：政策落地节奏不及预期风险，海外经济超预期波动风险。已公布的
宏观或产业政策在实际执行过程中，可能因配套措施不完善、部门协调不畅、
地方执行力度差异或市场环境变化等因素，导致其推进速度、覆盖范围或最终
效果慢于或弱于市场预期，从而影响政策目标的实现与经济修复的进程；主要
经济体的政治局势、经济数据或宏观政策出现意外变化，其幅度或频率超出市

场普遍预测。这种超预期波动可能引发全球金融市场动荡、贸易条件恶化或外
需急剧收缩，并通过金融、贸易及预期渠道向国内传导，增加我国外部环境的
复杂性与不稳定性。

分析师承诺 
本报告署名分析师具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格并注册为证券分析师，报告所采用的数据均
来自合法合规渠道，分析逻辑基于分析师的职业理解，通过合理判断得出结论，独立、客观地出具本报告。分析师承
诺不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意见或观点而直接或间接获取任何形式的补偿。

投资评级说明 
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为公司评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内
的相对市场表现，即：以报告发布日后 6 个月内公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅
作为基准。其中：A 股市场以沪深 300 指数为基准，新三板市场以三板成指（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针
对做市转让标的）为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准；美国市场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数为基准。 
公司评级 
买入：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 20%以上 
持有：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 10%与 20%之间 
中性：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于-10%与 10%之间 
回避：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于-20%与-10%之间 
卖出：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在-20%以下 
行业评级 
强于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报高于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 5%以上 
跟随大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报介于同期相关证券市场代表性指数-5%与 5%之间 
弱于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报低于同期相关证券市场代表性指数-5%以下

## Document 128: 261112_ubs_accton

### Page 1

ab 12 November 2026
Global Research
First Read
Accton Technology Corporation
Q4 GM to see strong recovery
Q3 GM  down 2.1pp QoQ on higher component cost
Accton reported Q326 EPS of NT$23.39, below UBS/Consensus estimates on softer
margins. Q326 sales of NT$120.7bn (up 26% QoQ) was already posted, and slightly
below UBSe as its  AI card business (Trainium 3) shifted to consignment model for certain
higher cost components from mid-August. Q326 GM of 17.6% was down 2.1ppts QoQ

due to   higher component cost and ~1.5 months of  consignment, and we believe this
should be temporary as its customer will reimburse them in the subsequent quarters. Q3
OPEX increased by  15% QoQ, primarily due to higher R&D expenses, resulted in OPM of
13.4% (-1.6ppts QoQ). We estimate data center switch accounted for ~37% of Q326
sales (vs 36% in  Q226), while accelerator card was ~50% of total sales (50% in Q226).
Q4  growth on track despite full quarter of consignment

Accton expects Q426F revenue to grow high-single digit % QoQ with potential upside
for low-teens % QoQ growth, subject to component supply (i.e. switch IC) and
customers' year-end inventory adjustment, despite a full quarter of component
consignment (vs 1.5 month in Q3). It sees robust demand from  cloud switch  with the
social media customer revenue contribution exceeded e-commerce customer from
Q326, which is likely to sustain into Q426F. It also noted the social media customer has

secured more switch chipsets from Marvell and Nvidia Spectrum, and continues to
negotiate with Broadcom for better allocation. Accton also sees better unit and content
growth for Trainium 3 as the customer ramps liquid-cooled design, which carry ~20%
higher ASP and better GM due to increased design complexity. We forecast Q426E sales
growth of 9% QoQ, while its GM should recover to 19.9% (+2.3 ppt QoQ) on more
favourable mix and its customer reimbursement on the Q3 prepaid component costs.

Aggressive capacity expansion to support mid-term  growth
Accton announced  US$226 mn (~NT$7.3 bn) new capex on 12 Nov. for capacity
expansion in Taiwan and Vietnam, as well as construction of new fab/cleanroom in
Malaysia and Singapore. It aims to fund the capex through internal cashflow, bank
loans,  and has announced a 10mn shares of rights issuance (~2% dilution) on 22 Oct.
We believe this will drive its overall capacity to grow 40-50% YoY in 2027E, supporting

overall 2027E revenue to grow ~35% YoY, driven by 1) further ramp of Trainium 3 with
higher adoption of liquid cooling, 2) sustained 800G switch demand and migration to
1.6T with new customer addition, 3) increasing contribution from Optical Switches.
Valuation: Reiterate Buy with NT$3,200 PT
We  fine tune our model and raise   2026-28E EPS by 0-2%. We retain  Buy but lower  PT to
NT$3,200 (from   NT$3,688) based on 22x  average 2027-28E PE (from 26x) on overall

market valuation compression. We believe recent concerns over switch IC  shortages and
GM pressure from higher component costs are overdone as we expect Accton's GM to
recover from Q426E. Accton will host its annual investor conference in late November.

Company Description
Founded in 1988, Accton specialises in ODM/OEM solutions for data center switches, AI
accelerator cards, enterprise/ODM switches, carrier access/telecom and others. Accton is one
of the largest vendors of white-box and OCP-compliant switches globally (by sales).

Leveraging deep expertise in high-speed switching, system integration and rapid time-to-
market, it has secured long-term partnerships with major cloud service providers.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Accton using PE methodology.
Key risks include: 1) weaker-than-expected demand for data center switches; 2) slower ramp
of 800G and 1.6T data center switches; 3) market share loss in the AI accelerator card
business; and 4) softer gross margin than expected.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Feedback: Read-across from
Three-Circle's comments for
Yageo
Positive pricing signals for Yageo: Chaozhou Three-Circle (300408.SZ/6951.HK,
not covered) spoke at our Asia Pacific Summit. It said that its 3Q26 MLCC ASPs rose
20–30% q/q on catch-up increases in mid/low-capacitance products. We see this as

supportive of firmer standard MLCC pricing at Yageo.
Tightness extends into 1H27: Three-Circle said its utilization rate was at 100% and
it expects high-capacitance shortages through 1H27. Management said Murata/
SEMCO’s capacity shifts were happening and this would continue to tighten
standard MLCC supply, supporting our view on Yageo (rated Overweight) and its
utilization and pricing.
Technology limits competitive overlap: Three-Circle said its  AI mix was single-digit,

with small, high-capacitance MLCCs still a capability gap. This suggests limited
immediate competition for the more demanding products within Yageo’s high-
capacitance portfolio.
Three-Circle said its >40% CY26 capacity growth outlook implies >70B MLCC
units/month by year-end, versus ~50B at the start of the year. This compares to
our estimate on Yageo at ~80B/month, although we don't know the exact
specifications used by either company, so that could limit direct capacity

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

高毛利產品合計來到 59%，此外營運槓桿帶來的效益使本業獲利

度恐受高利率環境而放緩，存在一定 AI 伺服器延宕部屬之風險，
因此研究部下修 PER 至 22x，目標價 2900 元，維持買進。

貿聯-KY(3665)成立於 1996 年，為全球連接線大廠，總部位於美
國矽谷，生產據點則廣泛分布在全球 37 地。產品應用包含數據通
訊、工廠自動化、半導體設備、醫療、車用等，2025 年工業佔 35%、
通訊 42%、車用 11%、電器 11%、其他 1%。銷售主要為美國 20%、
中國 20%、德國 16%、馬來西亞 7%、台灣 3%、其他 34%。

Interplex 正式併表，產品組合有望再度優化 
貿聯 10 月營收 118.5 億元，M+26.9%，Y+80.0%，自 9 月起完成
對 Interplex 的收購，10 月已完整貢獻營收，單月貢獻金額高達
6700 萬美金，約 20 億台幣，由於 Interplex 既有客戶與貿聯本身
有所重疊，然而產品項目卻有所不同，預計在透過集團整合能力下
將進一步推動公司在客戶群內滲透率的提升。此外貿聯本身新規格
的 Power 產品也持續提高出貨，研究部預期產品組合有望再度優
化，進一步推升獲利率表現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 26Q4 營
收 349.1 億元，Q+25.3%，Y+76.5%，毛利率 33.84%，稅後淨利
56.7 億元，Q+32.0%，Y+106.8%，EPS 28.39 元；2026 年營收
1069.3 億元，Y+50.1%，毛利率 31.83%，稅後淨利 152.1 億元，
Y+69.0%，EPS 76.23 元。

2027 年 HPC 產品維持價量齊揚，光通訊產品 2028 年有望發酵 
隨著 Vera Rubin 進入放量，Rack Busbar 升級至 5000A，Power 
Whip 規格從 60A 升級至 100A 且用量增加，1.6T AEC 也將逐步
開始貢獻營收，另外 Interplex 的加入所帶來的產品優化和客戶關
係的綁定，研究部認為貿聯 HPC 事業仍將迎來顯著的價量齊揚增
長。光傳輸領域上貿聯也持續耕耘，除了新富生 MPO 相關產品，
OCP 上與 ams Osram 的合作也意味著公司已著手布局下一代
Scale up 的通訊方案，研究部看好 wide-and-slow 架構在未來的導
入機會以及市場，預計 2028 年後的發酵有望帶來營運成長動能。
綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2027 年營收 1608.0 億元，Y+50.4%，

結論 
高毛利產品合計來到 59%，此外營運槓桿帶來的效益使本業獲利率創歷
史新高。Interplex 已完整併表，單月貢獻金額來到 6700 萬美金，除了
擴大產品線外，合併後同樣有助於加深既有客戶的合作關係。展望 2027
年，HPC 事業將迎來產品價量齊揚，尤其以高毛利的 Power 產品最為明
顯，根據客戶規劃，1.6T AEC 也將逐步起量，因此可預期 HPC 占比將
會再進一步提升，利於整體營運表現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2026
年 EPS 76.23 元，2027 年 EPS 131.41 元，目前 PE 為 11.6x，參考歷
史 PE 區間為 10-35x，考量貿聯獲利將維持高成長期，唯獨近期市場擔
憂資料中心建置速度恐受高利率環境而放緩，存在一定 AI 伺服器延宕部
屬之風險，因此研究部下修 PER 至 22x，目標價 2900 元，維持買進。

## Document 240: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시_dcb09b7c

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시_dcb09b7c.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-22

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성바이오로직스 (207940)

Company Brief 
제약/바이오 
Analyst 김준영 
junyoung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 김현비 
hyunbee.kim@meritz.co.kr 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요

3Q26 Review  
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(YoY +18.5%, QoQ +12.8%), 영업이익 
6,401억원(YoY +1.0%, QoQ +9.2%)으로 컨센서스를 각각 9.8%, 4.7% 상회했다. 
기존 1 ~ 4공장 풀가동 및 미국 Rockville 공장 매출 약 1,000억원과 5공장 PPQ 
물량 인식이 더해지며 외형 성장을 견인했다. 반면 신규 생산시설의 초기 비용 및 제
품 믹스 영향으로 OPM은 42.9%를 기록하며 전년 동기 대비 7.5%p 하락했다. 동
사는 연간 매출 성장률 가이던스를 기존 +15~20%에서 +20%로 상향했다. 당사는 
4분기 Rockville 공장 매출 기여와 기존 공장의 높은 가동률을 감안하여 연간 매출
액 5조 6,135억원(YoY +23.2%)을 예상한다.

신규 생산거점의 외형 확대, 이익 기여는 좀 더 긴 호흡으로 
5공장은 2Q26부터 PPQ 생산을 본격화했으나 상업 생산에 따른 실질적인 이익 기
여는 2027년 하반기에 확대될 전망이다. Rockville 공장은 3Q와 4Q 각각 약 
1,000억원의 매출이 예상되며 2027년 분기별 매출도 유사한 수준을 유지할 것으로 
전망한다. 다만 영업이익 기여까지는 시간이 더 소요될 것으로 예상되어 2027년 이
익 성장은 미국 공장보다 5공장 Ramp-up이 주도할 전망이다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원 유지 
삼성바이오로직스에 대해 투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원을 유지한다. 고객사 수주 
문의가 회복되는 가운데 대형 계약 협의가 진행 중이며 6공장 역시 연말 또는 내년 
초 착공을 목표로 하고 있다. 다만 신규 수주가 실제 계약으로 이어지는 시점과 5공
장의 상업 생산 전환 속도가 중기 실적의 핵심 변수로 판단한다. 추가 대형 수주 확
보 시 5공장 이후의 성장 가시성도 높아질 것으로 예상한다. 
PolyPeptide 인수는 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업을 확장한다는 점에서 긍정적이다. 인
수 완료 이후 다음 분기부터 연결 매출 인식이 가능하나 유의미한 영업이익 기여까지
는 최소 2년 이상 소요될 전망이다. PolyPeptide 인수는 현재 추정치에서 반영하지 
않았으며 향후 인수 완료 시 연결 OPM 희석 가능성을 감안할 필요가 있다. 한편 최
근 법원 항고심에서 배양부터 정제까지의 생산공정이 쟁의행위 기간에도 정상 수행되
어야 할 보전작업으로 인정되면서 파업에 따른 생산 차질 우려는 완화될 것으로 판단
한다. 노사 협상은 여전히 진행 중이나 생산 안정성에 대한 고객사 우려가 낮아질 경
우 신규 수주에도 긍정적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 22

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

표5  타법인 주식 양수결정: PolyPeptide Group AG 인수 개요 
항목 내용

## Document 245: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발_5484f7f5

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발_5484f7f5.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적 
2026-10-29

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 640,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 285,500 원 
상승여력: 124.2%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 감소 및 환율 하락으로 시장 예상치 하회 
Key: 4Q26~1Q27 부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 수주 모멘텀 전망 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 엔진&FDC 으로 밸류 매력도 충분. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 시장예상치 하회하는 실적 시현  
3Q26 연결 매출액 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영업이익 8,992억원
(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(9,792억원)를 하회했다. 조업일수 감소
(휴가 10 일+중대재해 4일) 및 환율 하락 영향(450 억원)과 더불어, 임단협 관련 예상 비
용 중 75%를 이번 분기 선반영 했기 때문이다. 상선 수주 빈티지는 중형 선박 기준 ‘23 년 
9.6%, ‘24년 65%, ‘25년 25%, 대형 선박 기준 ‘22년 4%, ‘23년 45%, ‘24년 31%, 
‘25년 20%로, 선가 상승효과 지속되는 중이다. 특히 해양 부문에서 루야 프로젝트의 공정
률 확대 효과 및 C/O로 180억원의 환입 발생으로 전 분기 대비 큰 폭의 이익 성장 시현했
다. 최근 키윗과 미국 내 합작 조선소 설립을 추진하며, 미 군수지원함 초도 2척 수주 및 향
후 물량 건조, 그리고 HII와의 함정 선체블록 하도급 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 64 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 82 만원에서 64 만원
으로 하향한다. 최근 상선 실적 피크아웃 우려에 따른 조선주 주가 급락으로 멀티플 하향 조
정했다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 
FDC 에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지속되고 있는 중이다. 올해 공시 기
준으로 육상발전 엔진 약 1.6GW 수주한 것으로 알려졌으나, 미공시된 약 1.4GW 까지 포
함 시 총 3.0GW 이상을 수주한 상황이다. 캐파 증설 기준으로 ‘28 년 1.5GW, ‘29 년 
3GW 캐파에 해당하는 물량을 이미 대부분 채웠으며, 기존 계획 대비 빠른 준공 혹은 공정
률 확대, 더 나아가 추가적인 캐파 증설까지 고려할 수준으로 파악된다. 빠르면 올해 연말 
혹은 내년 초부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 본격 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 
전망이다. 현 주가 레벨에서 상승여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 3Q 실적 및 임단협 불
확성 해소된 현 시점에서 적극적 매수 의견 및 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)로 제시한다.

## Document 281: Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms

Source PDF: all report pdf/Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Blue Wave: Democrats 
sweep the US midterms  
Summary 
Event: The Democrats gained control of the House of 
Representatives and the Senate at the US midterm elections 
that took place this week.  
Our View: With the US government now no longer under 
unified control of the Republicans, gridlock has returned to 
Washington. We believe investors should not overreact to 
these results and should stay invested. 
In equities, investors should continue to diversify their 
equity exposure into more defensive sectors. As we 
transition to mid-cycle for the equity markets, we expect 
stocks to continue to deliver positive returns in the 8-10% 
range per annum, a downshift from the torrid pace of gains in 
the earlier part of the cycle.  
In bonds, income remains attractive given recent spread 
widening. We favour non-hyperscaler investment grade 
corporate bonds at the three to seven-year part of the 
curve. We continue to expect the US dollar to remain range-
bound in the near term, as we expect two more rate hikes 
from the Fed, but as this mini rate hike cycle ends in the 
second quarter of 2027, the USD index (DXY) is likely to 
depreciate towards 98.5 as markets start to anticipate a Fed 
rate cut in the second half of next year.  
Oil is likely to rebound in the short-term on heightened 
geopolitical risk, but as supply chains adapt to the current 
situation and Congress exercises oversight on defense 
spending, WTI prices are likely to drift towards our 12-month 
target of USD 80 per barrel.

Democrats made solid gains across the Senate, House 
of Representatives and State Governors 
Results reported as at 7:45PM Singapore Time

Source: The Wall Street Journal, Standard Chartered

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 5 November 2026

Major implications of Democrat control of 
Congress   
1) Fiscal expansionary policies may slow. Democrats are 
now in position to block any new tax cuts or spending plans 
from the Trump administration. At the same time, any fiscal 
policies proposed by Democrats may also face Trump’s veto. 
Hence the fiscal trajectory of the US will likely remain stuck 
over the next two years. As we get into the second half of 
2027, the US debt ceiling will once again loom, and gridlock 
could potentially bring back government shutdowns. 
However, as they have always done in the past, compromise 
will ultimately prevail, in our opinion.  
2) Trump remains in control of foreign policy. With the 
midterms now out of the way, could Trump decide to re-start 
combat operations with Iran? We believe there is a risk that 
US and Israel – now that both countries’ have concluded 
important elections – will once again be tempted to restart 
combat operations to force an end to the conflict. However, 
the duration of any renewed hostilities may be limited by 
Congress next year as the Democrats will likely withhold 
funding for further military operations. In the near-term, oil 
prices may experience renewed volatility before stabilising at 
a lower level next year. The introduction of new tariffs on 
trading partners may prove harder with Democrat oversight. 
3) AI data centre moratoriums could grow, though model 
development regulatory changes would be harder to 
pass. While the headlines have focused on the House and 
Senate results, one important development has also 
occurred at the gubernatorial level. Notably, Democrats have 
won the governor races in Texas and Georgia, two big data 
centre states. As data centre build out permits are largely 
decided at the local state level, this will have important 
ramifications for build out plans next year. However, any 
Democratic proposals to regulate AI model developments 
would need to be signed off by President Trump, who 
continues to wield veto power on any such proposals.   
Asset class strategy 
Equities: Remain invested and continue to diversify.  
While investors initially overreacted, President Trump’s 
relatively conciliatory response has provided some welcome 
relief, helping markets recover strongly from their pre-market 
lows. Hence, we recommend investors avoid 
overreacting, remain invested and continue to diversify 
their equity exposure across sectors and regions.  
A few themes stand out from the early pre-market reaction to 
the mid-term elections. On the positive side, defensive stocks 
continue to fare well, while themes favoured by Democrats 
are also outperforming. Clean energy is a notable 
beneficiary, with solar stocks among the biggest winners

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation cycle 
is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle transition, 
where equity returns moderate but remain positive

today. Affordability-related themes are also rallying, including 
companies that could benefit from potential tariff rollbacks. 
Medicaid-related stocks are also moving higher on 
expectations that potential policy changes could provide a 
tailwind for the healthcare sector. 
On the negative side, semiconductor weakness continues 
amid concerns around potential data centre moratoriums. 
Our AI capex estimates remain conservative relative to 
consensus, but with a potential reset in expectations around 
data centre spending, investors should remain selective. 
After downgrading semiconductors last month, we have 
maintained a conservative stance on the industry, with a 
lower recommended allocation compared to September. 
While we believe the mid-term election outcome is not a 
complete surprise to markets, particularly given pre-election 
polling and the recent weakness in both the S&P 500 and 
Nasdaq, the overnight correction highlights how complacent 
investors had become, with the VIX initially surging more than 
10% in pre-market trading, although down from intra-day 
highs after President Trump’s comments. 
As we have highlighted in our recent notes on the transition 
to mid-cycle, this phase is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds. The mid-term election outcome 
reinforces the trend. The experience of 2015-2017, 
highlighted in the second table, offers a useful historical 
parallel. Despite the knee-jerk reaction, investors should not 
overreact based on one day’s moves. While we had trimmed 
equities to a core allocation last week, we continue to expect 
equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per annum 
during the mid-cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady underlying earnings growth in both 2027 
and 2028. 
So, what should investors do to navigate the next few months 
before the newly elected representatives are sworn in and 
take control of the House and Senate? As we have been 
highlighting in recent notes, equity markets should still deliver 
positive returns, albeit at a more moderate pace. Hence, we 
recommend staying invested but diversification remains key 
to navigating the next phase of the cycle.  
FX: DXY near-term support at 100.7, 12-month target at 
98.5. A Democratic sweep potentially removes the legislative 
runway for further tariff escalation and for fiscal expansion, 
the two structural pillars of US exceptionalism that have 
anchored dollar demand in 2026. Reduced fiscal impulse 
compresses the growth and yield differential that has kept the 
US dollar well-bid all year. Additionally, potential Democratic 
restrictions on AI and data centre investment could weigh on 
US equity outperformance, which is likely to act as a 
secondary dollar-negative channel.

DXY headwind from a Democratic sweep, the magnitude will

likely be modest and short-lived unless compounded by a

Fixed Income: Still attractive all-in yields; prefer 
investment grade corporates at three-to-seven year 
tenors to earn income and manage interest rate risk. The 
US Treasury bond market reaction appears to reflect a 
moderation in expectations of future economic growth after 
the election results. Yields at the intermediate end of the 
curve dropped the most, while the 30-year US yield 
moderated more modestly. We continue to expect two more 
Fed rate hikes between now and H1 2027 as still sticky 
inflation and the robust economy support further tightening.  
In the past few weeks, the US Treasury yield curve has 
broadly shifted downwards, after our opportunistic call to go 
long 10-year Treasuries at the end of September. We are of 
the view the ultra-long end downshift in yields is overdone 
(given the still challenging US fiscal trajectory) and would 
recommend investors who had earlier taken long duration 
exposures at the 20 to 30-year parts of the curve reduce their 
positions now. We continue to believe the 3 to 7-year part of 
the curve offers the most optimal balance of income and 
interest rate risk, particularly via investment corporate bonds. 
Within corporate credit, we expect a potentially slower pace 
of AI data centre buildouts could marginally improve market 
technicals, as hyperscaler and tech sector bond issuance 
may slow (and reduce “crowding out” of other bond 
issuances). Conversely, from a credit perspective, we believe 
this dynamic may also make the data centre focused deals 
less viable, and would be cautious on some of this sector’s 
lower quality names. Meanwhile, less AI capex dependent 
issuers continue to offer solid credit profiles amidst the still 
strong economy. High yield corporate spreads have widened 
in recent weeks but dispersion within the complex is wide, 
with BBs and Bs credit spreads staying relatively resilient, 
while CCCs have widened to distressed levels. For income-
focused investors, we would recommend staying within the 
crossover range of between BBB-BB.  
Commodities: As noted in the opening section, we believe 
there is a higher near-term risk of a restart of combat 
operations in Iran given the conclusion of the midterm 
elections. Given that, we believe there is a risk that oil 
prices could see a short-term spike, despite recent 
diplomatic overtures from Iran. However, for 2027 we 
continue to expect oil to stabilise at lower levels, as we 
think the Mideast situation should de-escalate given 
Democratic oversight of the defense budget, and the likely re-
configuration of oil supply chains. On the margin, we are also 
closely monitoring regulatory developments in the US shale

We expect oil prices to drift towards USD 80 a barrel as 
oil supply chains adapt, and Congress restrains the US 
defense budget

## Document 316: European Economics Analyst_ UK Outlook 2027_ Cyclical Headwinds, Strengthening Fundamentals

Source PDF: all report pdf/European Economics Analyst_ UK Outlook 2027_ Cyclical Headwinds, Strengthening Fundamentals.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-15
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The UK economy proved surprisingly resilient in 2026. Growth accelerated n
despite rising energy prices and tighter financial conditions, and the labour 
market showed signs of stabilisation. The new government navigated a difficult 
fiscal backdrop to deliver a low-key budget that maintained the consolidation 
path. 
The coming quarters are likely to be more challenging. First, we expect growth to n
slow as the impact of the energy shock on consumer spending increases over the 
winter. Second, inflation will rise further as electricity and gas bills increase and 
the indirect effects of the energy shock build. We expect this to push the BoE to 
hike a second time, probably in February. Third, the government faces a difficult 
Spending Review; existing spending plans look too tight, but the Chancellor has 
limited fiscal headroom. 
Nonetheless, we see compelling reasons to be constructive on the economic n
outlook for 2027. First, some of the recent growth improvement looks structural, 
and the productivity trend is strengthening. We therefore expect activity to 
reaccelerate later in the year as some of the cyclical headwinds fade, and our 
Q4/Q4 growth forecast is above consensus at 1.4%. Second, we do not expect 
strong second-round effects from the energy shock into domestic inflation, so 
the BoE is likely to hike significantly less than priced. Third, we expect further 
fiscal consolidation, and a more favourable OBR forecast could ease the 
trade-offs facing the Chancellor. 
We also see scope for sentiment around the UK’s longer-term economic n
prospects to improve. The UK is well-placed to benefit from AI adoption given its 
strengths in professional services, and surveys indicate that companies expect a 
material boost to productivity growth in the coming years. The PM has also 
recently raised the possibility of closer EU ties, which could bring notable 
economic gains over time.

### Page 2

UK Outlook 2027: Cyclical Headwinds, Strengthening Fundamentals

The UK economy proved surprisingly resilient in 2026. Despite the energy shock keeping 
inflation elevated and causing the BoE to pivot from cutting to hiking, growth 
accelerated this year. The labour market showed signs of improvement, with 
unemployment falling and employment growth indicators ticking up. The new 
government navigated a challenging fiscal backdrop to deliver a low-key Budget that 
maintained the consolidation path.

Cyclical Headwinds 
The coming quarters are likely to prove more challenging in several respects. First, we 
expect growth to slow in the near term and run slightly below trend in Q4 and the first 
half of 2027. The impact of the energy shock on household finances is likely to build over 
the winter, with weak real disposable income growth resulting in a slowdown in 
consumer spending.

Exhibit 1: Growth Proved Surprisingly Resilient in 2026

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bank of England
15 November 2026   2
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 3

Tighter financial conditions should also weigh on growth as mortgages are refixed at 
higher rates. Fiscal policy will likely turn more contractionary compared with this year 
given that current plans imply a larger consolidation in the primary balance in 2027.

While the labour market data improved over the last few months, we expect slower 
near-term growth to result in a modest further weakening. That is consistent with 
continued softness in most indicators of employment intentions. The pace of loosening 
should be gradual as the peak impact of last year’s labour cost increases on employment 
growth has probably passed. But we see the unemployment rate rising back to 5% by the 
middle of next year.

Exhibit 2: Consumer Spending Growth Likely to Slow

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics

Exhibit 3: Growth Headwinds from Rising Mortgage Rates and Tighter Fiscal Policy

Note that student loan figures for 2026-27 are affected by the accounting impact of a freeze in repayment thresholds, so the figures excluding student loans may 
give a better guide to the impacts on demand.

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg, OBR
15 November 2026   3
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 4

Second, inflation is likely to increase further in the coming months. Consumer energy 
prices will rise notably in January on the back of higher electricity and gas bills. We also 
expect food and core goods inflation to accelerate as the indirect effects of the energy 
shock become more apparent. We also see renewed upward pressure in some regulated 
services prices that are indexed to headline inflation, and higher fuel costs will likely raise 
transport services prices. Overall, we expect inflation to peak at 4.3% in February and 
average 4.1% in 2027H1.

The peak in headline inflation will likely exceed the threshold at which the BoE has 
typically become more concerned about second-round effects. Taken together with 
hawkish recent commentary, that points to another 25bp BoE hike to 4.25%. This will 
probably come in February, although an earlier December move is also possible.

Exhibit 4: Renewed Labour Market Softness

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bank of England

Exhibit 5: Rising Inflationary Pressures Point to a Second BoE Hike

The forecasts shown on the left are conditional on GS forecasts for energy prices.

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bank of England
15 November 2026   4
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 5

Third, the government faces a difficult Spending Review. Existing plans imply a notable 
slowdown in departmental spending growth from next year onwards, which looks 
challenging to deliver given pressures on the health and defence budgets. History 
suggests that these plans will probably be notably upgraded. New policy priorities could 
require further outlays, with PM Burnham’s agenda likely to become clearer after the 
publication of his 10-Year Plan. But financing higher spending may be challenging given 
limited fiscal headroom, with revenues constrained by Labour’s manifesto not to raise 
taxes on working people. Welfare reforms could bring some savings over time but look 
likely to be accompanied by upfront investment in employment support.

Structural Improvement 
Despite these cyclical challenges, we see several reasons to be more constructive on the 
UK’s economic prospects for 2027. First, some of the tailwinds that supported growth 
this year look structural. Strength in business investment has partly reflected rising 
information and communications capex, which looks likely to be sustained by ongoing 
data centre investment. This investment should support continued growth in technology 
services output, where demand has increased on the back of AI adoption. Further 
growth in utilities capital spending linked to renewables should also continue to support 
demand.

Exhibit 6: A Challenging Spending Review

Source: OBR
15 November 2026   5
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 6

The productivity trend also appears to be improving, as growth in output per worker has 
increased significantly recently. Some of this may reflect the impact of last year’s 
employer National Insurance hike on hiring, but more recent data also show gains in 
sectors such as professional services that were less affected by labour cost increases. We 
also see more fundamental reasons to expect a pickup. While Brexit has probably 
weighed significantly on growth over the past decade, the worst of the headwind has 
likely passed. Survey data from the Decision Maker Panel indicate that early gains from 
AI adoption could also have modestly boosted the figures.

These factors, together with declines in energy prices, should mean that growth 
reaccelerates through the second half of next year. Overall, we expect a 1.4% Q4/Q4 
increase in GDP, above consensus expectations as well as the BoE and OBR forecasts.

Exhibit 7: Some Growth Drivers Look More Structural

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Yotzov et al. (2026)

Exhibit 8: Trend Productivity Growth is Probably Improving

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Various Academic Studies
15 November 2026   6
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 7

Second, we continue to expect limited second-round effects into domestic price 
pressures. Even after the recent decline in the unemployment rate, broader measures of 
utilisation (such as the vacancy/unemployment ratio) suggest that the labour market 
remains far looser than in 2022/23. That will likely reduce the extent to which higher 
headline inflation passes through into wage pressures. Indeed, early data indicate that 
pay settlements are likely to remain stable next year, and the BoE’s Decision Maker Panel 
survey shows little change in wage expectations since the start of the Middle East 
conflict. Private sector regular pay growth will probably accelerate from low levels but is 
likely to remain well below the levels seen after the previous energy shock.

With only a modest increase in pay growth, we expect overall labour cost inflation to be 
well below the levels seen in recent years. This is especially true in consumer-facing 
sectors, which saw sharper increases in wages over recent years and the largest impact 
from the employer National Insurance increases. More moderate labour cost inflation

Exhibit 9: Our GDP Forecast is Slightly Above Consensus

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg, Bank of England, OBR

Exhibit 10: Second-Round Effects Likely to be Moderate

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bank of England
15 November 2026   7
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 8

should help to keep a lid on underlying services inflation, despite cost pressures from 
higher energy and food prices.

Given moderate second-round effects, we think that the BoE will hike less than priced. 
After tightening policy in February, we expect the MPC to keep Bank Rate at 4.25% for 
the rest of the year, whereas the market implies two further rate increases. The risks 
around our baseline are skewed towards further tightening, but even on a 
probability-weighted basis our forecast is notably below current market pricing.

Third, 2027 is likely to see renewed progress on deficit reduction. The current fiscal 
plans imply a steep reduction in the primary deficit next year. While we think spending 
could overshoot the official projections, tax receipts could also come in somewhat 
higher. Further consolidation should mean that the UK continues to compare favourably 
to other advanced economies in terms of reducing borrowing. While the Spending 
Review does pose a challenge for the government, it is possible that changes in the

Exhibit 11: More Limited Labour Cost Increases Should Keep a Lid on Underlying Services Inflation

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, ONS

Exhibit 12: We Expect the BoE to Hike Less Than Priced

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Bloomberg
15 November 2026   8
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 9

economic forecast could help the Chancellor. The OBR’s wage growth forecast continues 
to look low, and an upgrade would boost the tax revenues projection, increasing fiscal 
space.

A Positive Long-Run Outlook 
We also see scope for sentiment around the UK’s longer-term economic prospects to 
improve. The UK is well-placed to benefit from AI adoption given its specialisation in 
high-value added services. IMF estimates indicate that a relatively high share of the 
workforce is exposed to AI, with a particularly large number of workers in roles that may 
see productivity gains but are less likely to be displaced. Survey data suggest that firms 
expect adoption to result in sizeable productivity increases in the coming years. Even 
though these gains are unlikely to be fully additive to the current productivity trend, the 
numbers still indicate upside risks to our estimates for supply growth.

Exhibit 13: Continued Fiscal Consolidation

On the left, the UK data are shown on a public sector basis.

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, OBR, Bank of England
15 November 2026   9
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 10

The UK is also well-positioned in several high growth innovative industries. This includes 
a world-leading position in life sciences, such as pharmaceuticals, biotechnology, 
medical technology, and genomics. The UK has a world-class research base, with the 
London/Oxford/Cambridge triangle a leading biotech cluster. 
Another possible source of long-run growth upside would be closer ties with the EU. The 
upcoming (November 20) summit could see some progress, with the two sides expected 
to finalise agrifood and emissions trading deals. But the Prime Minister has also 
indicated that he will use the summit to outline options for how the relationship might 
develop after the next election, potentially including a customs union, rejoining the 
single market, or even a second referendum on membership. Any reset would require 
negotiation, political trade-offs, and time before benefits materialise. But it could bring 
significant economic upside, so signals towards improved trading links could start to lift 
optimism on the UK’s growth prospects.

Exhibit 14: Well-Placed to Benefit from AI Adoption

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, IMF, Bank of England

Exhibit 15: Signals Towards Closer EU Ties Could Boost Sentiment on Long-Run Growth

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Various Academic Studies, Dhingra and Sampson (2019)

## Document 317: European resilience, American vigour (2026-10-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/European resilience, American vigour (2026-10-30).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

European resilience, American vigour

Eurozone growth continues to prove resilient, notwithstanding the volatility of the Irish figures. The Q3 data reinforce 
our forecast of 1.1% growth this year, which could even be slightly exceeded, as German and Spanish momentum shows 
no sign of fading. In France, the negative surprise (-0.1% q/q against a consensus of +0.1% q/q) stems from a new one-
off factor (the sharp fall in aerospace output caused by delays in input deliveries) and masks an encouraging rebound 
in household consumption. In the United States, growth reached its highest level since Q2 2025 (0.8% q/q), driven by 
buoyant private domestic demand.

Eurozone: The resilience of growth is confirmed 
Eurozone GDP grew by 0.4% q/q in Q3 according to Eurostat's preliminary flash estimate, above both the consensus and our 
Nowcast (0.3%). The ever-volatile Irish figures contributed 0.2 pp to this outcome. Excluding Ireland, growth (0.2% q/q) 
nonetheless remains in line with the pace of recent quarters (0.2-0.3% q/q on average). This illustrates the Eurozone's capacity 
to withstand the current shocks, thanks in particular to the strength of Spain (+0.07 pp contribution to the q/q change in Eurozone’s 
GDP) and the Netherlands (+0.04 pp), and to Germany's gradual recovery (+0.06 pp). France (-0.03 pp) and Italy (zero growth), 
by contrast, posted disappointing results, although in France's case these partly reflect temporary factors (aerospace output). The 
growth carry-over for 2026 now stands at 1.0%, which supports our forecast of 1.1% in annual average terms and even points to 
upside risk. The European Commission's October surveys remain upbeat, with a further improvement in industrial activity 
expectations. 
The German recovery continues. As anticipated by our Nowcast, real GDP grew by 0.2% q/q in Q3 (after 0.3% in Q2). 
According to Destatis, consumption (private and public) increased slightly, while investment edged down. Export momentum 
slowed markedly compared with Q2. This is Germany's fourth consecutive quarter of growth. 
Italian GDP surprised on the downside, stagnating in Q3 (against a consensus of 0.1%). According to Istat, this reflects a 
decline in value added in industry, offset by increases in services and agriculture. The positive contribution from domestic demand 
was reportedly cancelled out by a negative contribution from net trade. 
Spanish growth remained robust in Q3, in line with expectations (0.6% q/q, after 0.7% in Q2), driven mainly by domestic 
demand (0.5 pp contribution) and, to a lesser extent, by net exports (0.1 pp). Consumption remains the main engine of growth 
(+0.6% q/q for private consumption and +0.9% for public consumption). Investment rose by 0.7%. Export growth (1.3%) once 
again outpaced import growth (1.1%) this quarter. All sectors except construction recorded positive changes in value added, with 
industry and services both up 0.7%. 
France: A sharp drop in aerospace output prevents a return to growth in Q3 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 (after stagnating in Q2), weighed down by a sharp fall in aerospace output (-4.5% q/q 
in the production of other transport equipment, which includes aerospace). This steep decline dragged down goods production 
(down 1.2% q/q) and explains the sharp contraction in exports (-1.7% q/q) and business investment (-0.4% q/q). Net trade thus 
subtracted 0.6 pp from GDP (imports were flat over the same period). Conversely, household consumption surprised on the 
upside (+0.4% q/q), boosted by purchases of electric vehicles (registrations up 112% y/y in Q3). Public consumption 
(+0.3% q/q) also supported growth, as did activity in market services (+0.3% q/q). Despite the sharp drop in aerospace 
output in Q3, Airbus has confirmed its delivery targets for the year (with engine supply problems expected to be resolved), pointing 
to a strong rebound in growth in Q4 (0.3% q/q according to our forecasts). Overall, growth is expected to reach 0.3% in 2026, 
before picking up to 0.8% in 2027. 
United States: Growth at a one-year high  
GDP growth picked up in Q3 to 3.4% at a seasonally adjusted annualised rate (AR; i.e. 0.8% q/q), from 2.2% in Q2 
(0.6% q/q). This is the fastest pace since Q3 2025, confirming that activity is almost immune to the energy shock. Private domestic 
demand remains particularly strong. Private non-residential investment accelerated to 12.7% AR, a performance unmatched since 
the post-Covid reopening. AI-related components are driving this figure (18.5% AR), but other types of investment are also rising 
for a second consecutive quarter (3.8% AR) after contracting uninterruptedly throughout 2025. Household consumption, steady 
at 2.5% AR in real terms, is growing faster than it did before inflation picked up again, but at the cost of a significant drop in the 
saving rate (3.8% at the end of the quarter, down from 4.4% at the end of the previous quarter). The United States nonetheless 
continues to "import its growth": net trade is weighing on GDP owing to a surge in imports (19.7% AR), driven by AI-related 
components – IT, semiconductors and telecommunications (103.8% AR).

熱門議題
► 黃金價格歷經第二季修整，近日呈現大幅反
彈走勢，主要係因美國期中選舉後，市場憂
心AI產業監管將趨嚴，加上晶片與科技股遭
遇大幅修正，促使部分資金轉往黃金等防禦
型資產尋求避險。
► 根據世界黃金協會最新報告，第3季全球央行
淨買進321公噸黃金，較第二季明顯回升，年
增率達42%，創下自2024年第四季以來的單
季新高，前3季累計買進金額更是高達675公
噸，顯示黃金儲備已經列入全球央行必需資
產配置而且比重維持穩定增長態勢。
► 長期以來黃金具有抗通膨特性，觀察黃金與
聯邦基金利率走勢，從2020年之後，即便全
球歷經2次升息循環，黃金價格仍呈現穩健上
揚走勢，反應其抗通膨保值特性。此外，美
國財政赤字壓力難消，恐令美元長期購買力
被稀釋， 加 速 全 球 央 行 去 美 元 化 ， 有 鑑 於
此，建議黃金納入資產配置。

► 醫療類股於美國第三季財報展現強勁的獲利
韌性，實際獲利優於預期程度為主要產業中

禮來、再生元製藥、吉利德科學)在減重藥、
癌症治療及創新藥領域之需求持續增長，帶
動整體產業獲利表現優於市場預期。
► 期中選舉後醫療政策輪廓逐漸明朗。民主黨
主張支持擴大醫療保險覆蓋、延續平價醫療
法案補貼以及緩和醫療補助計劃(Medicaid)
削減，有助提升醫療服務使用率；雖藥價談
判政策恐帶來部分定價壓力，但整體政策主
張仍對創新藥、生技研發及醫療保險產業具
潛在政策支持效果。
► 綜上，在AI投資回報率面臨市場檢驗之際，
市場資金持續尋求防禦性配置。醫療保健類
股與資訊科技類股相關性偏低，有助分散投
資組合對單一AI題材依賴，加上第三季獲利
動能轉強、創新藥需求穩健及產業能見度提
升，後續有望持續受惠資金輪動效應。

支持延長《平價醫療法案》補貼、擴
大醫療補助計劃(Medicaid)覆蓋範圍，
並緩和該補助計劃的削減
傾向由州政府及市場機制主導醫療
體系，限制聯邦政府支出
醫療的可負擔性為兩黨政策共同焦點，有助
增加就醫人口及處方藥使用量，支撐製藥、
醫療服務及保險業長期需求

投資焦點必需消費
► 短期而言，檢視各類股在最近股災表現，美
國成長股、資訊科技、與通訊服務等高波動
度類股，表現明顯落後大盤，其中，資訊科
技與通訊服務波動度最大、跌幅最重，主要
係因美股科技巨頭財報不如預期、負自由現
金流量，疊加期中選舉後民主黨恐緊縮AI基
礎建設進程及資本支出見頂等不確定因素，
令市場 擔憂 導致 資 金流出 ；並轉 進核 心消
費、健康護理、防禦股、公用事業等防禦性
產業，報酬相對較穩健，受市場資金青睞。
► 長期而言，從2008迄今，核心消費類股長期
表現穩健上漲。與主要指數相比，面對近月
AI科技股修正，同期間費半大跌18%、那斯
達克跌幅6%、標普跌幅1%，而核心消費類
股大漲13%，反應其低波動、抗震韌性。
► 整體而言，從2015年至今，歷經2次通膨與
升息循環，核心消費受影響性低，有鑑於資
產配置角度，建議短期跟隨資金流向配置核
心消費股，提高投組抗震韌性。
必需消費長期表現穩健、抗通膨、低波動避險資金流入，必需消費類股單月表現最佳

股市
 隨著美國期中選舉結束，美伊衝突可望降溫，市場關注焦點將回歸基本面，惟AI資料中心建設進程
或將受到州政府緊縮政策而造成評價面壓力增添股價波動。短期可布局防禦股、分散配置於不同國
家與產業，增強抗震力、降低市場波動影響，長期而言，AI從全面看好變成要追求財務健全度較高
的品質股、且大優於小，震盪之後長期正向趨勢不變。
 歐、日股可分散配置，利率走升有利銀行股。此外，隨財政政策支持，歐洲國防軍工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，AI從
全面看好變成要追求財務健全度較高的品
質股、且大優於小，震盪之後長期正向趨
勢不變，非AI部分可關注必需消費產業、
醫療保健產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲國防
債市
 隨資金湧入，公債殖利率回落，惟根據最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升
息空間，立場偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨發展。建議以中短天期債券投資為主，可逢利率走升
時布局進行鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳，具穩定現金流與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引

力者，包含金融、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、通訊及公用事業等
匯市
 聯準會12月份會議升息態度偏鷹，美元高檔整理。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期偏鷹，日圓弱勢盤整。
美元高檔整理，日圓弱勢盤整
商品
 美國期中選舉結束，預期中東地緣衝突降溫，油價從近期高點回落，短線仍偏震盪。
 去美元化支撐全球央行買盤，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
低接買盤流入，黃金低點反彈，中長期維
持偏多看法

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書(或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用(境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金(不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施)；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

## Document 370: Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 23 October 2026

EcoFlash

Q3 Growth in advanced economies: solid despite shocks

Growth looks to have held up in Q3 despite successive shocks (see our analysis), as it did in 2025 and H1 2026. A longer 
Middle East conflict has fed into more persistent inflation; drought has worsened in many countries, and interest rates 
have risen further – at the short end, with both the ECB and the Fed raising policy rates in September, and even more at 
the long end. However, our nowcasts show that growth is bending without breaking in most advanced economies. GDP 
figures (due on 29 October in the US and 30 October for the euro area and its member states) are expected to confirm 
that. In the Euro area, momentum looks stable (excluding Ireland, whose GDP is volatile). In Germany, growth has been 
uninterrupted since Q4 2025, while France is likely to have returned to growth after stagnating in H1. In the US, growth 
is likely to have gathered pace, according to the GDP Now, still driven by AI, but increasingly by other sectors as well.

Euro area: resilience beneath the surface. Our nowcast points to growth slowing to 0.3% in q/q, though the pace is stable 
excluding Ireland (Irish GDP is volatile and had markedly boosted the Q2 figure). Excluding Ireland, growth should hold at its H2 
average, a sign of the bloc’s underlying resilience. Also, momentum has broadened to a growing number of sectors (see our 
analysis). While industrial production stalled over the summer, services remained buoyant. The services PMI rebounded sharply 
in Q3 and sits firmly in expansion territory (53.0 in September). Improving job-related survey data (PMI and European Commission 
surveys) and a stable unemployment rate (6.4% in August) hint at more hiring in Q3 – a support for household consumption that 
partly offsets purchasing-power losses from persistent inflation. Corporate investment is underpinned by buoyant new investment 
lending (8.7% y/y In August, 12-month cumulative). 
France: growing, nevertheless. The succession of shocks does not appear to have prevented France from returning to 
growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q, according to our nowcast. One-off factors accounted for the stall in activity in H1. In Q3, the drought 
worsened and industrial production remained subdued. However, market services production rebounded in July and August, 
notably in business services, restaurants, digital and real estate. The latter points to residential investment recovering after a weak 
H1. Corporate investment likely followed, as reflected in buoyant credit flows and AI-related investment. Household consumption 
is likely to have improved slightly on higher electric vehicle sales.  
Euro area  France

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas  Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas

Germany: growth still solid. The H1 rebound in activity is moderating but continues. Our nowcast points to a fourth straight 
quarter of growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q (after 0.3% in Q4 2025, 0.4% in Q1 2026, and 0.3% in Q2). Manufacturing activity eased 
slightly, according to the September PMI survey (53.9, down 0.4 m/m). Government spending will remain growth-supportive, with 
defence and infrastructure outlays gaining traction. Infrastructure spending continues to underpin activity in public works, which 
stands at its highest since November 2021 (2.5% 3m/3m in August). Exports (4.4% y/y between January and August) remain the 
main growth engine, helped by a pivot towards Europe (7.2%). 
United States: an expansion undeterred by inflation. Growth is likely to have gathered pace in Q3. The Atlanta Fed’s GDP 
Now points to a 3.5% annualized growth rate (AR), close to our own 3.7% forecast (0.9% non-AR) and up from 2.2% in 
Q2. Domestic demand remains the engine. Consumption is weathering the pickup in inflation, while the AI-driven capex cycle 
remains robust – as reflected in retail sales and nondefence capital-goods orders (excl. aircraft). As a result, growth in final sales

### Page 2

to private domestic purchasers1 should print above 4% AR again. By contrast, external trade should remain a drag on growth, 
reflecting AI-related import needs. Survey data point to momentum that is both solid and broad-based. The ISM indices have 
remained firmly in growth territory (54.9 quarterly average for manufacturing and services alike). At the same time, they highlight 
persistent price and supply-chain pressures.

Germany   United States

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas   Source: Refinitiv, Atlanta Fed, BNP Paribas

## Document 409: US MARKETS CALL_ Below The Calm Deep Blue Sea

Source PDF: all report pdf/US MARKETS CALL_ Below The Calm Deep Blue Sea.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US MARKETS CALL: Below The Calm Deep Blue Sea
Date:    2 hours ago
US MARKETS CALL: Below The Calm Deep Blue Sea

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US MARKETS CALL: Below The
Calm Deep Blue Sea
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 14 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Thomas Vimare / Unsplash

The major stock market indexes have gone nowhere since early August.
On the surface, the seas have been calm. Underneath, plenty of
turbulence has been occurring. Most of the volatility has been within
sectors.
In mid-September, we warned our readers to proceed with caution through
the midterm elections. We expected and are now seeing buying

### Page 2

opportunities. The S&P 500 closed at 7,561.67 on Friday. It has been
fluctuating around 7,600 since August 3. We think it is set for another rally
that should take it up to 7,900 by the end of this year.
I. Stock Market Performance
The S&P 500 is down just 0.5% since August 3, while the equal-weight
version of the index is up 1.8% (chart). The MAGS ETF is up 0.4%, while
the XMAGS ETF is up 1.2%. The Nasdaq is down 3.0%. Only the Russell
2000 has moved much, falling 6.4%.

The sectors underneath the S&P 500 have been choppier (chart). Health
Care has gained 16.4% since August 3, and Consumer Staples is up
10.8%. Communication Services is down 11.3%, and Industrials is down
7.9%. Information Technology, at the center of the AI selloff, is down 3.5%.

### Page 3

The Information Technology sector has also been extremely turbulent
below the surface (chart). The Semiconductor Equipment and
Semiconductors industries are down 23.9% and 13.6% since August 3.
Meanwhile, IT Consulting is up 22.3%. Application Software and Systems
Software are up 12.0% and 4.5%. The crosscurrents have largely offset
each other.
The Magnificent-7 stocks have diverged too (chart). Nvidia’s market cap
has dropped to $3.92 trillion following news of CEO Jensen Huang’s

resignation. Apple’s market cap is at a record $5.18 trillion. Alphabet’s fell
after reporting earnings but has since stabilized at $3.62 trillion.
II. Stock Market Earnings

### Page 4

Industry analysts are usually too optimistic about the earnings of the
companies they follow. As quarterly earnings reporting seasons approach,
they are often forced to cut their estimates. That's evident in their annual
consensus "Earnings Squiggles," which tend to start high and then fall to
the reported results (chart). This time, the annual Earnings Squiggles for
2026, 2027, and 2028 all are rising. That’s highly unusual. It might reflect
some irrational exuberance among analysts. Then again, they've gotten

more optimistic as actual earnings have come in stronger than anticipated
in recent quarters.
The recent market turbulence hasn’t dented S&P 500 operating earnings-
per-share estimates. The 2027 consensus rose to $427.01 last week, and
the 2028 consensus rose to $496.24 (chart). Both are new highs. Forward
earnings hit another record high at $420.15 per share, edging closer to the
2027 estimate. We are estimating $425 for next year.

### Page 5

Q3’s consensus earnings growth estimate rose again, to 26.9% y/y from
25.9% a week earlier (chart). Q4’s estimate edged up to 29.8%. The Q2
actual earnings growth soared on mark-to-market accounting gains.
Without them, earnings rose about 25% rather than 50.4%.
III. Stock Market Valuation
Valuation multiples continue to fall. The S&P 500’s forward P/E is down to
18.0, while the Magnificent-7’s is down to 21.4 (chart). We think the
contraction may be close to a bottom. Our year-end S&P 500 target

implies a forward P/E of 18.6. Multiples usually shrink on recession fears.
We don’t see a recession coming. Expectations of a slowdown in the AI
buildout may be weighing on valuations. In our view, the slowdown
constitutes speed bumps, not roadblocks.

### Page 6

Only 36.1% of S&P 500 stocks now trade above a forward P/E of 20,
down from nearly 50% earlier this year (chart). That’s a healthy
development, driven by the air coming out of the AI bubble, which is now
less likely to burst.
The multiple correction has been centered in S&P 500 Growth, not Value,
stocks. The S&P 500 Growth forward P/E has dropped 27% to 19.0 from
26.0 at the start of this year, while Value is at 18.0, about the same as at
the beginning of the year (chart).

### Page 7

The spread between the Growth and Value forward P/Es has narrowed to
just 1.0 point, the narrowest since 2010 (chart). Investors aren’t paying up
for growth anymore.
Only three sectors have gotten more expensive this year: Real Estate,
Consumer Staples, and Health Care (chart). Information Technology’s
forward P/E has dropped from 26.5 at the end of last year to 17.2.
Communication Services’ forward P/E is down from 22.1 to 17.0.

### Page 8

IV. Credit
The 10-year Treasury bond yield jumped to 5.10% after October's CPI and
PPI reports (chart). That’s still below its October peak above 5.30%.
The 10-year yield is hovering near the top end of the 4.00%-5.00% range
we consider normal (chart).

### Page 9

The latest increase in the 10-year nominal yield has been attributable to
the 10-year TIPS yield. The spread between the two, which is a proxy for
10-year expected inflation, remains low at 2.21% (chart). That's surprising
since the recent yield rise followed the hotter-than-expected CPI and PPI
reports.
Credit default swap spreads rose in recent months on concerns about the
debt of CoreWeave and other AI borrowers but have since narrowed
(chart). We don’t see broadening credit stress.

### Page 10

V. Commodities
Commodity prices are signaling less inflationary pressure. The FIBER
Commodities Spot Price Index is looking toppy (chart).
The S&P GSCI Energy Spot Index also seems to be topping out along
with oil prices (chart).

### Page 11

Even diesel prices have come down substantially. The Gulf Coast spot
price fell to $4.11 a gallon from a peak above $5.00 earlier this year
(chart).
Agricultural prices have also eased from their summer highs (chart).

### Page 12

The copper price has been getting hit along with the AI trade. It has fallen
to $6.08 a pound from its recent record high above $6.80 (chart).

featured in our segment, "Ed Answers Your Questions".

## Document 418: What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity continues to turn the screw_ (2026-10-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity continues to turn the screw_ (2026-10-22).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

The bank
for a changing

WHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM
THE MEASURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO
TURN THE SCREW?
Marianne Mueller and Pascal Devaux
issue 19
21 October 2026
The 2022 energy crisis clearly highlighted the issue of electricity prices in Europe — and, more broadly, the issue of energy prices. As
a result, European manufacturers’ competitiveness has been severely undermined when compared with the United States and China.

By reigniting tensions over electricity prices, the conflict that began in the Middle East in late February 2026 further underscores the
continent’s vulnerability to fossil fuel imports, despite the expansion of renewable energy. Faced with an environment of potential sharp
and sudden price rises, European governments do not have the same room for manoeuvre.
While the additional costs borne by European manufacturers are mainly linked to the share of gas in national electricity mixes, we will

first illustrate that, in Germany, grid operation and maintenance costs, as well as taxation, are also contributing significantly to these
costs.
We will then explore the solutions proposed by the German government under the new European measures regulating state aid to
electricity-intensive industries.
Finally, our estimates will show that, while these measures make Germany more competitive against some European countries, they
do not place it on an equal competitive footing with the United States or China. Nevertheless, Germany has made significant progress

in decarbonising and making its electricity mix more flexible from a European perspective. This should help to ensure that electricity costs
are reduced in the medium term.

### Page 2

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 19 / 21 October 2026

for a changing
worldWHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM THE MEA- SURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO TURN THE SCREW?
GAS: A KEY FACTOR IN THE ADDITIONAL ELECTRICITY COSTS
FACED BY EUROPEAN INDUSTRY
European manufacturers are still paying significantly higher elec -
tricity costs than their American and Chinese competitors. In 2019,

there was a 30% difference between the prices charged to European
electricity-intensive industries (corresponding to the lower end of the
industrial price range) and the average US industrial price. In 2025,
it stood at nearly 100% according to the IEA (2026) 1, after peaking at
an average of 148% between 2022 and 2023. There is currently a 50%
difference with their Chinese competitors2.
The disruption to Russian gas supplies since 2022 is behind the rising

gas prices, particularly as the EU has had to turn to liquefied natural
gas3 (LNG), which is more expensive than gas transported via pipe -
line and, above all, exposes Europe to global gas market conditions.
The growth of LNG has, in fact, intensified competition between the
major consumption regions, primarily Europe and Asia. European and
Asian prices now tend to move more in tandem (Chart 1). However, in
China, the prevalence of coal — a widely available local resource —

and a more regulated electricity price help to keep industrial prices
lower. At the same time, the United States enjoys plentiful domestic
gas, which is produced at a low cost, driven by the sharp rise in shale
oil and gas production over the past decade. By contrast, since 2022,
Europe has been more exposed to the cost of imported gas (90% of its
total consumption, according to Eurostat), while the United States and
China have enjoyed plentiful domestic resources.

Fluctuations and the level of gas prices are passed on to Eu -
ropean electricity prices via the ‘merit order’ marginal pricing
mechanism, with the wholesale price determined by the margi -
nal cost of the last (and most expensive) electricity source called
upon to meet demand.
As a result, gas plays a decisive role, with gas-fired power sta -
tions setting the price in around 40% of cases in Europe4, with si-
gnificant variations depending on national electricity mixes. It is

close to this average in Germany, below it in Spain (26%) and
France (17%), and above it in Italy (73%) 5 in 2025 6 7. Therefore,
electricity prices are still sensitive to fluctuations in gas prices,
even when electricity is not generated from gas (Chart 2). This
pass-through is particularly pronounced when demand rises
and requires the use of more expensive sources, or when supply
constraints arise (for example, during extreme heatwaves that
reduce wind-power generation or disrupt the cooling of nuclear

power plants).
1 Prices – Electricity 2026 – Analysis – IEA
2 The difference may be even larger for other manufacturers who do not receive the same support as electricity-intensive sectors.
3 Imports of Russian gas (via pipeline and LNG) accounted for around 45% of total European imports in 2021, compared with 14% in Q2 2026 (Bruegel, natural gas imports).
4 ACER, 2026, Key developments in EU electricity and gas markets, 2026 Monitoring report.
5 In Italy, in response to sharply rising gas prices in 2026, the government is currently negotiating a potential option of capping the prices of gas supplied to power stations in

order to limit the rise in the wholesale price of electricity. However, this can only be a temporary general mechanism, with the potentially major cost being borne by public funds
and/or households.
6 Eurelectric, 2026, Power Barometer.
7 EMBER, 2026, Latest energy shock reminds Europe of its risky reliance on gas.
8 Energy shock: 2026 vs. 2022 dashboard – 2nd October 2026 – Economic Studies – BNP Paribas
However, for the time being, the 2026 energy crisis is having a far

smaller effect on electricity prices than in 2022. On the one hand, the
2026 peak reached by the European TTF benchmark is still well below
that recorded in 20228.
In addition, there has been significant progress in decarbonising Eu -
ropean electricity mixes since 2022, which has helped to mitigate, on
average, the pass-through of gas prices to electricity prices.

SOME EUROPEAN COUNTRIES HAVE MANAGED TO STAY
COMPETITIVE, BUT GERMANY IS STILL THE EXCEPTION
Electricity prices partly vary due to countries’ electricity mixes
(Chart 3). Electricity-intensive manufacturers in countries where the
share of fossil energy is high face higher costs (Poland, Italy and Ire -
land) (Chart 4). Conversely, countries with a less carbon-intensive mix

(particularly France and Spain in recent times) are less directly ex -
posed to these fluctuations, with costs closer to the United States and
China (Chart 5).
The situation in Germany is paradoxical, as, despite progressively
greening its electricity mix and wholesale electricity prices (averaging
90 EUR/MWh over the 2023–2025 period) and average electricity costs
(across all industrial sectors) in line with the European average, the most
energy-intensive industries face higher costs than the European average

(and even more so compared with international competitors). This is
mainly due to grid costs and a tax burden above EU standards (Chart 6).
9 Eurostat data are used to compare prices with the rest of the EU. However, given that electricity prices are not uniform for businesses at a European level, there may be differences
between Eurostat and national sources. Jäger P., 2025, “Cautionary tale or model for Europe? How member states should react to Germany’s new electricity subsidy”, Hertie School,

Jacques Delors Centre.
10 P. Devaux, 2026, “European Union: low carbon transition and energy sovereignty, a path fraught with obstacles”
In the first half of 2026, for the most electricity-intensive industries,
the difference compared with the European average was 9%, based on
the prices excluding taxes and levies provided by Eurostat. This gap is
mainly due to the cost of accessing the grid. If all taxes and levies are
included, the difference rises to 21% (Chart 7)9.

EUROPE’S APPROACH OF ADOPTING BOTH STRUCTURAL
PROGRAMMES AND TARGETED SUPPORT MEASURES
European programmes to support the low-carbon transition pursue
objectives relating to climate action (reducing greenhouse gas emis -
sions), sovereignty10 (increasing energy self-sufficiency) and competi -
tiveness (reducing the volatility and costs associated with fossil fuels).
The RePowerEU programme, which was launched in 2022 in response
to the energy crisis, primarily aims to reduce imports of Russian gas

and, more generally, to accelerate the low-carbon transition. Although
Europe has reduced its dependence on imports of Russian fossil fuels,
there has still not been enough progress around the energy transition
and cross-border connectivity. This is due to governance constraints
